When is his marvellous wealth made known? The sense is that no one knows the time of its bestowal. And when would he be for the protection (ऊती (*ūtī*)) and the prosperity of the worshipper who praises and sacrifices?

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कः वीरः (*kaḥ vīraḥ*)—which hero
- अस्य (*asya*)—with this Indra
- सधमादं (*sadhamādaṃ*)—the helpful companionship in battle
- आप (*āpa*)—obtains?
- कः (*kaḥ*)—which man of merit
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- सुमतिभिः (*sumatibhiḥ*)—with the favourable thoughts
- समानंश (*samānaṃśa*)—joins?
- कत् (*kat*)—when
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—the attractive wealth
- चिकिते (*cikite*)—is it known?
- कत् (*kat*)—when
- शशमानस्य (*śaśamānasya*)—of the one who praises
- यज्योः (*yajyoḥ*)—and performs the sacrifice, the sacrificer
- वृधे (*vṛdhe*)—for prosperity
- ऊती (*ūtī*)—and for protection
- भुवत् (*bhuvat*)—does (Indra) become manifest?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Which hero, in time of battle, obtains the companionship of this Indra, who gives help in battle? Which man of merit gains the favourable thoughts of this Indra? When is this Indra's attractive wealth made known? When does Indra become manifest for the prosperity and the protection of the sacrificer, who praises and performs the sacrifice?

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
What hero has enjoyed his fellowship (in battle)? who has been a sharer in his benevolent thoughts? when does any one apprreciate his wonderful bounty ? when is he (present) for the promotion of the prosperity of the man who glorifies and worships him?

### Printed p. 119

#### Special Topics
The meaning of this Ṛk is clear. In this Ṛk and in some of the following Ṛks the seer describes the qualities of Indra in the form of questions. Since their meaning is easy and clear, no further explanation is necessary.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒था शृ॑णोति हू॒यमा॑न॒मिंद्रः॑ क॒था शृ॒ण्वन्नव॑सामस्य वेद ।  
> का अ॑स्य पू॒र्विरुप॑मातयो ह क॒थैन॑माहुः॒ पपु॑रिं जरि॒त्रे ॥

> *kathā śṛṇoti hūyamānamiṃdraḥ kathā śṛṇvannavasāmasya veda |*  
> *kā asya pūrvirupamātayo ha kathainamāhuḥ papuriṃ jaritre ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कथा । शृणोति । हूयमानं । इंद्रः । कथा । शृण्वन् । अवसां । अस्य । वेद ।  
> काः । अस्य । पूर्विः । उपमातयः । ह । कथा । एनं । आहुः । पपुरिं । जरित्रॆ ॥ ३ ॥

> *kathā | śṛṇoti | hūyamānaṃ | iṃdraḥ | kathā | śṛṇvan | avasāṃ | asya | veda |*  
> *kāḥ | asya | pūrviḥ | upamātayaḥ | ha | kathā | enaṃ | āhuḥ | papuriṃ | jaritrè ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रः परमैश्वर्ययुक्तो हूयमानमाह्वयंतं स्तोतारं कथा कथं
> शृणोति । स्तोत्राणि शृण्वन्नस्य स्तोतुः संबंधीन्यवसां रक्षणानि कथा कथं च वेद ।
> वेत्ति । पूर्विः पुरातनान्यस्येंद्रस्य संबंधीन्युपमातयो दानानि का ह । कानि चित्
> संतीति शेषः । तानि दानानि जरित्रॆ स्तोतुः संबंधिनां कामानां पपुरिं पूरयितारमेनमिंद्रं कथा कथं चाहुः । वदंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, endowed with supreme lordship: how does he hear the worshipper who calls (invokes) him? And hearing the praises, how does he know the protections (अवसां (*avasāṃ*)) belonging to this praiser? Which are the ancient gifts (उपमातयः (*upamātayaḥ*)) of this Indra? The words "there are some" are to be supplied. And how do they speak of this Indra, as the fulfiller (पपुरिं (*papuriṃ*)) of the desires of the praiser?

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra, endowed with lordship
- हूयमानं (*hūyamānaṃ*)—the praiser who praises and calls (his praise)
- कथा (*kathā*)—in what manner
- शृणोति (*śṛṇoti*)—does he hear
- शृण्वन् (*śṛṇvan*)—after hearing this praise
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this praiser, the desired
- अवसां (*avasāṃ*)—protections
- कथा (*kathā*)—in what manner
- वेद (*veda*)—does he know
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- पूर्विः (*pūrviḥ*)—ancient
- उपमातयः (*upamātayaḥ*)—gifts
- काः ह (*kāḥ ha*)—of what kind are they
- जरित्रॆ (*jaritrè*)—the desires wished for by the praiser
- पपुरिं (*papuriṃ*)—who fulfils
- एनं (*enaṃ*)—this Indra
- कथा (*kathā*)—in what manner
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—do they praise?

### Printed p. 120

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

How does Indra, endowed with great lordship, hear the praise of the praiser who praises him? Having been praised in this manner, how does he come to know what protections are desired by the praiser in return for those praises? Of what kind are the ancient gifts of this Indra? In what manner can the Hotṛs praise Indra, who fulfils the desires wished for by the praiser?

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
How is it that Indra hears (the worshipper) who invokes him? and hearing, how does he know his necessities? what are his gifts of old; why have they termed him the fulfiller (of the desires) of him who offers praise?

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒था स॒बाधः॑ शशमा॒नो अ॑स्य॒ नश॑द॒भि द्रवि॑णं॒ दीध्या॑नः ।  
> दे॒वो भु॑व॒न्नवे॑दा म ऋ॒तानां॒ नमो॑ जगृ॒भ्वा अ॒भि  ॑
> यज्जुजोषत् ॥ ४ ॥

> *kathā sabādhaḥ śaśamāno asya naśadabhi draviṇaṃ dīdhyānaḥ |*  
> *devo bhuvannavedā ma ṛtānāṃ namo jagṛbhvā abhi*  
> *yajjujoṣat ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कथा । सबाधः । शशमानः । अस्य । नशत् । अभि । द्रविणं । दीध्यानः ।  
> देवः । भुवत् । नवेदाः । मे । ऋतानां । नमः । जगृभ्वान् । अभि । यत् ।  
> जुजोषत् ॥ ४ ॥

> *kathā | sabādhaḥ | śaśamānaḥ | asya | naśat | abhi | draviṇaṃ | dīdhyānaḥ |*  
> *devaḥ | bhuvat | navedāḥ | me | ṛtānāṃ | namaḥ | jagṛbhvān | abhi | yat |*  
> *jujoṣat ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सबाधः सपत्ना नां पीडासहितः शशमान इंद्रविषयां स्तुतिं कुर्वन् दीध्यानः
> कर्मभिर्दिप्यमानो यजमानोऽस्येंद्रस्य संबंधि द्रविणं पश्वादिधनं कथा
> कथमभि नशत् । अभिप्राप्नोति । नमो हविर्लक्षणमन्नं जगृभ्वान् गृहीतवानिंद्रो

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificer is "accompanied by affliction" (सबाधः (*sabādhaḥ*)), that is, harassed by his rivals; he is "praising" (शशमानः (*śaśamānaḥ*)), that is, offering praise addressed to Indra; and he is "shining" (दीध्यानः (*dīdhyānaḥ*)) through his rites. How does he obtain this Indra's wealth (द्रविणं (*draviṇaṃ*)), the wealth of cattle and the like? नमः (*namaḥ*) is the food that has the nature of an oblation; Indra is जगृभ्वान् (*jagṛbhvān*), "one who has taken" it.

### Printed p. 121

> यद्यदाभि जुजोषत् मामभिसेवेत तदा देवो दीप्यमान इंद्रो मे मदीयानामृतानां स्तोत्राणां नवेदा अतिशयेन ज्ञाता भुवत् । भवेश् । अत्र
> निपातस्थो नकारोऽतिशयवाची ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When Indra, having taken the oblation, favours me (जुजोषत् (*jujoṣat*)), then may that shining Indra become the exceedingly good knower (नवेदाः (*navedāḥ*)) of my ṛtas, that is, of my praises. भुवत् (*bhuvat*) is optative ("may he be"). Here the न (*na*) that stands among the particles denotes excess.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शशमानः (*śaśamānaḥ*)—the one who praises Indra
- दीध्यानः (*dīdhyānaḥ*)—and shines through rites; the sacrificer
- सबाधः (*sabādhaḥ*)—though oppressed by enemies
- अस्य (*asya*)—belonging to Indra
- द्रविणं (*draviṇaṃ*)—wealth such as cattle
- कथा (*kathā*)—in what manner
- अभि नशत् (*abhi naśat*)—does he obtain
- नमः (*namaḥ*)—the food in the form of the oblation
- जगृभ्वान् (*jagṛbhvān*)—Indra who has taken
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अभि जुजोषत् (*abhi jujoṣat*)—he is served before me
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—that same venerable Indra
- मे (*me*)—offered by me
- ऋतानां (*ṛtānāṃ*)—the praises
- नवेदाः (*navedāḥ*)—as one who knows
- भुवत् (*bhuvat*)—let him be.

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

How does the sacrificer, who praises Indra and shines through rites, though oppressed by enemies, obtain the wealth of cattle and the like belonging to Indra? When Indra, who has taken the food in the form of the oblation, turns towards me and is served, then may that same venerable Indra be the knower of the praises offered by me.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
How does he who glorifies Indra, and is diligent in his worship, although encountering opposition, obtain from him wealth? may the divine Indra be the appreciator of my praises, when accepting the sacrificial food he is propitiated towards me.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒था कद॒स्या उ॒षसो॒ व्यु॑ष्टौ दे॒वो मर्त॑स्य स॒ख्यं जु॑जोष ।  
> क॒था कद॑स्य स॒ख्यं सखि॑भ्यो॒ ये अ॑स्मि॒न्नामं॑ सु॒युजं॑  ॒
> ततस्रे ॥ ५ ॥

> *kathā kadasyā uṣaso vyuṣṭau devo martasya sakhyaṃ jujoṣa |*  
> *kathā kadasya sakhyaṃ sakhibhyo ye asminnāmaṃ suyujaṃ*  
> *tatasre ‖ 5 ‖*

### Printed p. 122

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कथा । कत् । अस्याः । उषसः । विउष्टौ । देवः । मर्तस्य । सख्यं । जुजोष ।  
> कथा । कत् । अस्य । सख्यं । सखिभ्यः । ये । अस्मिन् । कामं । सुयुजं । ततस्रे ॥ ५ ॥

> *kathā | kat | asyāḥ | uṣasaḥ | viuṣṭau | devaḥ | martasya | sakhyaṃ | jujoṣa |*  
> *kathā | kat | asya | sakhyaṃ | sakhibhyaḥ | ye | asmin | kāmaṃ | suyujaṃ | tatasre ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> देवो द्योतमान इंद्रोऽस्या उषसो व्युष्टौ प्रभाते मर्तस्य मर्त्यस्य
> मनुष्यस्य स्तोतुः संबंधि सख्यं सखिकर्म कथा कथं केन प्रकारेण कत्कदा
> जुजोष । सेवेत । ये स्तोतारोऽस्मिन्निंद्रे सुयुजं सुष्टु प्रयुज्यमानं
> कामं कामनीयं हविः स्तोत्रं च ततस्रे वितेनिरे अस्येंद्रस्य सख्यं सखिकर्म
> सखिभ्यः स्तोतृभ्यः कथा कथं कत्कदाचित् । भवेदिति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The shining Indra, at the dawning (व्युष्टौ (*vyuṣṭau*)) of this dawn, in the morning: how, in what manner, and when does he accept (जुजोष (*jujoṣa*), "would serve") the friendship (सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*), the office of a friend) of the mortal (मर्तस्य (*martasya*)), the praiser, a human being? The praisers who have spread out (ततस्रे (*tatasre*)) towards this Indra the well-applied, desirable oblation and praise: how and when does this Indra's friendship come to those friends, the praisers? The words "may it be" are to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवः (*devaḥ*)—Indra, the shining one
- आस्त्याः उषसः (*āstyāḥ uṣasaḥ*)—of this dawn
- व्युष्टौ (*vyuṣṭau*)—at the time of rising
- मर्तस्य (*martasya*)—to help the praiser, a human being
- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—the office of a friend
- कथा (*kathā*)—in what manner
- कत् (*kat*)—when
- जुजोष (*jujoṣa*)—does he carry out
- ये (*ye*)—which praisers
- अस्मिन् (*asmin*)—addressed to this Indra
- सुयुजं (*suyujaṃ*)—that which is to be offered excellently
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—the oblation (or the praise)
- ततस्रे (*tatasre*)—have extended and spread out (such)
- सखिभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ*)—to the praisers, the friends of Indra
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—the helpful act of friendship
- कथा (*kathā*)—in what manner
- कत् (*kat*)—when (does it become manifest)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In what manner and when does the shining Indra, at the time of the rising of this dawn, carry out his office of friend in order to help the human praiser? When and in what manner does the friendly act of Indra become manifest to those praisers who have spread out and offered, addressed to this Indra, the excellently offered oblation or praise?

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
when, in what manner, at the dawning of this morning, has the divine Indra accepted the friendship of a mortal? when, and in what manner, (is) his friendship (manifested) to the friends who have spread out the desirable and suitable (offering) before him ?

### Printed p. 123

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> किमादम॑त्रं स॒ख्यं सखि॑भ्यः क॒दा नु ते॑ भ्रा॒त्रं प्र ब्र॑वाम ।  
> श्रि॒ये सु॒दृशो॒ वपु॑रस्य॒ सर्गाः॒ स्व१॒॑र्ण चि॒त्रत॑ममिष॒  
> आ गोः ॥ ६ ॥

> *kimādamatraṃ sakhyaṃ sakhibhyaḥ kadā nu te bhrātraṃ pra bravāma |*  
> *śriye sudṛśo vapurasya sargāḥ svarṇa citratamamiṣa*  
> *ā goḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> किं । आत् । अमत्रं । सख्यं । सखिभ्यः । कदा । नु । ते । भ्रात्रं । प्र । ब्रवाम ।  
> श्रिये । सुदृशः । वपुः । अस्य । सर्गाः । स्वः । न । चित्रतमं । इषे । आ । गोः ॥ ६ ॥

> *kiṃ | āt | amatraṃ | sakhyaṃ | sakhibhyaḥ | kadā | nu | te | bhrātraṃ | pra | bravāma |*  
> *śriye | sudṛśaḥ | vapuḥ | asya | sargāḥ | svaḥ | na | citratamaṃ | iṣe | ā | goḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वयं यजमानास्ते त्वदीयममत्रं शत्रुनभिभावुकं सख्यं सखिकर्म सखिभ्यः स्तोतृभ्यः किमात् किं स्वित् प्र ब्रवाम । प्रकर्षण वदेम । ते
> त्वदीयं भ्रात्रं भ्रातृकर्म च कदा नु कदा खलु प्र ब्रवाम । सुदृशः शोभनदर्शनस्यास्येंद्रस्य संबंधिनः सर्गा उद्योगाः श्रिये सर्वेषां स्तोतृणां श्रेयसे ।
> भवंतीति शेषः । स्वर्ण सूर्यो यथा दर्शनीयतमस्तथा गोर्गंतुरिंद्रस्य
> चित्रतमं दर्शनीयतमं वपुः शरीरमेषे । आ समंतात्सर्वैरिष्यते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, we sacrificers: your friendship (सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)), which overpowers (अमत्रं (*amatraṃ*)) enemies, to the friends, the praisers: may we in some way (किमात् (*kimāt*), "in what way indeed") proclaim it forth? And your brotherly act (भ्रात्रं (*bhrātraṃ*)): when indeed may we proclaim it?" The endeavours (सर्गाः (*sargāḥ*)) of this fair-looking Indra are for the welfare (श्रिये (*śriye*)) of all the praisers; "they are" is to be supplied. As the sun (स्वः (*svaḥ*)) is most beautiful to behold, so the most wonderful (चित्रतमं (*citratamaṃ*)), most beautiful form (वपुः (*vapuḥ*)) of Indra the goer (गोः (*goḥ*)) is wished for (आ इषे (*ā iṣe*)) on all sides by all.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- अमत्रं (*amatraṃ*)—destroying enemies
- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—your friendly act
- सखिभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ*)—to the praisers, your friends
- किमात् (*kimāt*)—in any way at all
- प्र ब्रवाम (*pra bravāma*)—may we declare it with praise
- ते (*te*)—your
- भ्रात्रं (*bhrātraṃ*)—brotherly act
- कदा नु (*kadā nu*)—when is it to be praised
- सुदृशः (*sudṛśaḥ*)—of excellent form
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- सर्गाः (*sargāḥ*)—all the activities
- श्रिये (*śriye*)—are the cause of the welfare (of all praisers)
- स्वः न (*svaḥ na*)—radiant like the sun
- गोः (*goḥ*)—of Indra who moves
- चित्रतमं (*citratamaṃ*)—most attractive
- वपुः (*vapuḥ*)—the body
- आ इषे (*ā iṣe*)—is esteemed by all

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, may your friendly act, which destroys enemies, be declared in some way with praise by your friends to the praisers? When is your brotherly act to be praised?

### Printed p. 124

The acts of this Indra, of excellent form, are all a cause of the welfare of all the praisers. The most attractive body of Indra, who moves radiant like the sun, is esteemed by all.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May we in any manner proclaim your friendship for your friends? when may (we make known) your fraternal regard? the efforts of thc well-looking Indra are for the happiness of all : the wonderful form of moving (Indra) is, like the sun, ever wished for.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> द्रुहं॒जिघां॑सन्‌ध्व॒रस॑मनिं॒द्रां तेति॑क्ते ति॒ग्मा तु॒जसे॒ अनी॑का।  
> ऋ॒णा चि॒द्यत्र॑ ऋण॒या न॑ उ॒ग्रो दू॒रे अज्ञा॑ता उ॒षसो॑  ॒
> बबाधे ॥ ७ ॥

> *druhaṃjighāṃsandhvarasamaniṃdrāṃ tetikte tigmā tujase anīkā|*  
> *ṛṇā cidyatra ṛṇayā na ugro dūre ajñātā uṣaso*  
> *babādhe ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> द्रुहं । जिघांसन् । ध्वरसं । अनिंद्रां । तेतिक्ते । तिग्मा । तुजसे । अनीका ।  
> ऋणा । चित् । यत्र । ऋणयाः । नः । उग्रः । दूरे । अज्ञाताः । उषसः । बबाधे ॥

> *druhaṃ | jighāṃsan | dhvarasaṃ | aniṃdrāṃ | tetikte | tigmā | tujase | anīkā |*  
> *ṛṇā | cit | yatra | ṛṇayāḥ | naḥ | ugraḥ | dūre | ajñātāḥ | uṣasaḥ | babādhe ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रो द्रुहं द्रोहं कुर्वतीं ध्वरसं हिंसिकामनिंद्रामिंद्रमजानतीं राक्षसीं जिघांसन् हंतुमिच्छन् तिग्मा तिग्मानि पूर्वमेव तीक्ष्णान्यनीकानीकान्यायुधानि तुजसे तद्वधाय तेतिक्ते । अत्यर्थं तीक्ष्णीकरोति । यत्र यासूषःसु
> तदुपलक्षितेश्वहस्त्वणा चित् ऋणान्यपि नोऽस्यान्बाधंत इति शेषः । ता उषसश्चोग्र
> उद्धूर्णबल ऋणया ऋणस्य हंतेंद्रः । यातिर्वधकर्मा । दूरेऽज्ञाता अननुभूताः सतीर्बबाधे । अपीडयत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra, wishing to slay (जिघांसन् (*jighāṃsan*)) the demoness who does harm (द्रुहं (*druhaṃ*)), who injures (ध्वरसं (*dhvarasaṃ*)) and who does not know Indra (अनिंद्रां (*aniṃdrāṃ*)), sharpens exceedingly (तेतिक्ते (*tetikte*)) for her slaying his weapons (अनीका (*anīkā*)), already sharp (तिग्मा (*tigmā*)). Then, in the dawns in which even debts (ऋणा (*ṛṇā*)) afflict us, "they afflict us" being the words to be supplied, the fierce (उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)) Indra, of mighty strength, the slayer of the debt (ऋणयाः (*ṛṇayāḥ*)), afflicted those dawns, which were unknown, unexperienced, far away; *yātiḥ* is a word for the act of slaying. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 125

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- द्रुहं (*druhaṃ*)—(the Rākṣasī) who practises treachery
- ध्वरसं (*dhvarasaṃ*)—who is a doer of violence
- अनिंद्रां (*aniṃdrāṃ*)—who does not worship Indra; such a Rākṣasī
- जिघांसन् (*jighāṃsan*)—wishing to kill
- तिग्मा (*tigmā*)—(already) sharp
- अनीका (*anīkā*)—weapons
- तुजसे (*tujase*)—to kill her
- तेतिक्ते (*tetikte*)—made still sharper
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which dawn-times (in which days)
- ऋणा चित् (*ṛṇā cit*)—debts also (like enemies)
- नः (*naḥ*)—torment us; such
- उषसः (*uṣasaḥ*)—dawn-times (such days)
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—the one of fearsome strength
- ऋणयाः (*ṛṇayāḥ*)—the destroyer of debts, Indra
- दूरे (*dūre*)—very far away
- अज्ञाताः (*ajñātāḥ*)—so that they do not come within our ken
- बबाधे (*babādhe*)—has pushed away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Wishing to kill the Rākṣasī who practises treachery, does violence and does not worship Indra, Indra made his weapons, already sharp, still sharper for her destruction. The days on which debts too torment us like enemies, those days Indra, of fearsome might and destroyer of debts, has pushed far away so that they do not come within our ken. That is, he has protected us so that we never groan under the burden of debt.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Resolving to kill the oppressing, malevolent (Rakshasi), not acknowledging Indra, he sharpened his sharp weapons for (her) destruction and the fierce (Indra), the canceller of debts, has kept afar the unknown dawns in which the debts (are to be paid).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

द्रुहं ध्वरसं अनिंद्रां जिघांसन् तिग्मा अनीका तुजसे तेतिक्ते (*druhaṃ dhvarasaṃ aniṃdrāṃ jighāṃsan tigmā anīkā tujase tetikte*)—
अयमिंद्रः द्रोहं कुर्वतीं हिंसिकां इंद्रमजानतीं राक्षसीं हंतुमिच्छन् पूर्वमेव तीक्ष्णानि आयुधानि तद्वधाय अत्यर्थं तीक्ष्णीकरोति । (*ayamiṃdraḥ drohaṃ kurvatīṃ hiṃsikāṃ iṃdramajānatīṃ rākṣasīṃ haṃtumicchan pūrvameva tīkṣṇāni āyudhāni tadvadhāya atyarthaṃ tīkṣṇīkaroti |*). That is: in order to kill a Rākṣasī who behaves with treacherous intent towards others, causes violence, and hates Indra when she sees him, Indra made his weapon still sharper although it was already sharp. Who the Rākṣasī spoken of here is, is not clear. She may be a woman of evil nature belonging to the Dasyu race, who caused violence to the seer-folk.

### Printed p. 126

That the word ध्वर (*dhvara*) has the meaning "violence" Yāska states by the etymology ध्वरतिर्हिंसाकर्मा (*dhvaratirhiṃsākarmā*) (Ni. 1-8). Although the word तिग्मं (*tigmaṃ*) is read among the names of the thunderbolt-weapon (Ni. 3-11), that the word तिग्म (*tigma*) has the sense "sharp" Yāska shows by citing the Ṛk
इमा रुद्राय स्थिरधन्वने गिरः क्षिप्रेषवे देवाय स्वधान्ने (*imā rudrāya sthiradhanvane giraḥ kṣipreṣave devāya svadhānne*)
अषाळ्हाय सहमानाय वेधसे तिग्मायुधाय भरता शृणोतु नः (*aṣāḷhāya sahamānāya vedhase tigmāyudhāya bharatā śṛṇotu naḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-46-1)
and explaining the word तिग्मायुधाय (*tigmāyudhāya*) in it as तीक्ष्णायुधाय (*tīkṣṇāyudhāya*) (Ni. 10-7). The word तुजः (*tujaḥ*) too is read among the names of the thunderbolt (Ni. 3-11). Here, holding that it is formed from the root तुज हिंसायां (*tuja hiṃsāyāṃ*), he has given for the word तुजसे (*tujase*) the meaning "for killing".
The meaning of the sentence यत्र ऋणाचित् नः उषसः उग्रः ऋणयाः दूरे अज्ञाताः बबाधे (*yatra ṛṇācit naḥ uṣasaḥ ugraḥ ṛṇayāḥ dūre ajñātāḥ babādhe*) is not clear. Debts (loans), which like enemies torment the mind, cause us distress always, that is every day, as the dawn-times come every day. Indra has removed their burden; that is, the intention is that by giving us wealth and the like he has done us the kindness of freeing us from the harassment of creditors.

> ऋ॒तस्य॒ हि शु॒रुधः॒ संति॑ पू॒र्वीर्यु॒तस्य॑ धी॒तिर्वृ॑जि॒नानि॑ हंति ।  
> ऋ॒तस्य॒ श्लोको॑ बधि॒रा त॑तर्द॒ कर्णा॑ बुधा॒नः शु॒चमा॑न  ॒
> आयोः ॥ ८ ॥

> *ṛtasya hi śurudhaḥ saṃti pūrvīryutasya dhītirvṛjināni haṃti |*

> ऋतस्य । श्लोकः । बधिरा । ततर्द । कर्णा । बुधानः । शुचमानः । आयोः ॥ ८ ॥

> *ṛtasya | hi | śurudhaḥ | saṃti | pūrviḥ | ṛtasya | dhītiḥ | vṛjināni | haṃti |*  
> *ṛtasya | ślokaḥ | badhirā | tatarda | karṇā | budhānaḥ | śucamānaḥ | āyoḥ ‖ 8 ‖*

### Printed p. 127

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतपेयनाम्मेकाहे मरुत्वतीयनिष्केवल्यशस्त्रयोर्निविद्धानात्पुरस्तादृतस्य हीत्येतदादिके द्वे ऋचौ क्रमेण शंसनीये । सूत्रितं च । ऋतस्य हि शुरुधः
> संति पूर्विीरिति सूक्तमुखीये । आ. ९-७ । इति ॥
> अत्र ऋतशब्देनेंद्रो वादित्यो वा सत्यं वा यज्ञो वोच्यते ॥ ऋतस्य
> ऋतदेवस्य संबंधिन्यः पूर्विर्बह्वः शुरुध आपः संति हि । भवंति खलु । ऋतस्य
> ऋतदेवस्य संबंधिनी धीतिः प्रज्ञा तद्विषया स्तुतिर्वा वृजिनानि वर्जनीयानि पापानि
> हंति । हिनस्ति । ऋतस्य ऋतदेवस्य संबंधिनी श्लोकः स्तुतिरूपा नागायोर्गंतुर्मनुष्यस्य संबंधिनौ बधिरा बधिरौ प्रतिबद्धौ कर्णा कर्णौ ततर्द । तृणत्ति । कीदृशः
> श्लोकः । बुधानो बुध्यमानः शुचमानो दीप्यमानः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Ekāha sacrifice called Ṛtapeya, the two Ṛks beginning ऋतस्य हि (*ṛtasya hi*) are to be recited in order, before the nivid, in the Marutvatīya and Niṣkevalya śastras. It is also laid down in a sūtra: "ऋतस्य हि शुरुधः संति पूर्विीरिति (*ṛtasya hi śurudhaḥ saṃti pūrviīriti*) in the sūktamukhīya" (Ā. Sū. 9-7).
Here by the word ऋत (*ṛta*) is meant Indra, or Āditya, or truth, or sacrifice. The many waters (शुरुधः (*śurudhaḥ*) = waters) belonging to Ṛta, the god Ṛta, exist indeed. The धीतिः (*dhītiḥ*) of Ṛta—understanding, or praise directed to him—destroys sins, that is, what is to be shunned. The श्लोकः (*ślokaḥ*) of Ṛta, in the form of praise, pierces open the closed, deaf ears of the moving man (आयोः (*āyoḥ*) = man). **NOTE** What kind of praise? One that is awakening (बुधानः (*budhānaḥ*) = being known) and shining (शुचमानः (*śucamānaḥ*) = blazing).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—belonging to the god Ṛta
- शुरुधः (*śurudhaḥ*)—waters
- पूर्विः (*pūrviḥ*)—of many kinds
- संति हि (*saṃti hi*)—are they not well known?
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—relating to the god Ṛta
- धीतिः (*dhītiḥ*)—understanding (or praise concerning him)
- वृजिनानि (*vṛjināni*)—sins
- हंति (*haṃti*)—destroys
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—addressed to the god Ṛta
- बुधानः (*budhānaḥ*)—filled with understanding
- शुचमानः (*śucamānaḥ*)—shining
- श्लोकः (*ślokaḥ*)—the praise
- आयोः (*āyoḥ*)—of man
- बधिरा (*badhirā*)—deaf
- कर्णा (*karṇā*)—ears
- ततर्द (*tatarda*)—opens (gives the power of hearing)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

It is well known that the waters belonging to the god Ṛta are of many kinds. Praise addressed to Ṛta destroys sin. Praise addressed to Ṛta, filled with understanding and shining, opens man's deaf ears and gives them the power of hearing.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Many are the waters of Rita : the adoration of Rita destroys iniquities ; the intelligent and brilliant praise of Rita has opened the deaf (ears) of man.

### Printed p. 128

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the bhāṣya of this Ṛk Sāyaṇa says: अत्र ऋतशब्देनेंद्रो वादित्यो वा सत्यं वा (*atra ṛtaśabdeneṃdro vādityo vā satyaṃ vā*) यज्ञो वोच्यते (*yajño vocyate*). That is, the word ऋत (*ṛta*) in this Ṛk and in the next two Ṛks indicates the deity denoted by the word ऋत (*ṛta*). This deity, he says, may be any one among the deities Indra, Āditya, Satya, Yajña. For the word ऋत (*ṛta*) Yāska gives the etymology सत्यं वा (*satyaṃ vā*) यज्ञं वा (*yajñaṃ vā*). Since subjects such as water, rain, food and praise are in question here, all of these can be applied to any of the deities mentioned above.
In the sacrifice called ऋतपेय (*ṛtapeya*), performed for one day only, the nivid-sūkta is recited at the beginning of the recitation of the mantras of the Marutvatīya and Niṣkevalya śastras. Before that nivid-sūkta the two Ṛks of this sūkta beginning ऋतस्य हि (*ṛtasya hi*) (the 8th and 9th Ṛks of this sūkta) are to be recited in order, that is, one Ṛk before each nividdhānīya-sūkta. This is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana-śrauta-sūtra ऋतस्य हि शुरुधः संति पूर्विरिति (*ṛtasya hi śurudhaḥ saṃti pūrviriti*) सूक्तमुखीये (*sūktamukhīye*) (Ā. Sū. 9-7).

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒तस्य॑ दृ॒ळ्हा ध॒रुणा॑नि संति पु॒रूणि॑ चं॒द्रा वपु॑षे॒ वपूं॑षि ।  
> ऋ॒तेन॑ दी॒र्घमि॑षणंत॒ पृक्ष॑ ऋ॒तेन॒ गाव॑ ऋ॒तमा वि॑वेशुः ॥

> *ṛtasya dṛḷhā dharuṇāni saṃti purūṇi caṃdrā vapuṣe vapūṃṣi |*  
> *ṛtena dīrghamiṣaṇaṃta pṛkṣa ṛtena gāva ṛtamā viveśuḥ ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋतस्य । दृळ्हा । धरुणानि । संति । पुरूणि । चंद्रा । वपुषे । वपूंषि ॥  
> ऋतेन । दीर्घं । इषणंत । पृक्षः । ऋतेन । गावः । ऋतं । आ । विवेशुः ॥ ९ ॥

> *ṛtasya | dṛḷhā | dharuṇāni | saṃti | purūṇi | caṃdrā | vapuṣe | vapūṃṣi ‖*  
> *ṛtena | dīrghaṃ | iṣaṇaṃta | pṛkṣaḥ | ṛtena | gāvaḥ | ṛtaṃ | ā | viveśuḥ ‖ 9 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वपुषे वपुष्मतः । षष्ठ्यर्थे चतुर्थी । ऋतस्य ऋतदेवस्य संबंधीनि दृळ्हा
> दृढानि धरुणानि धारकाणि पुरूणि बहूनि चंद्रा चंद्राण्याह्लादकानि वपूंषि रूपाणि
> संति । भवंति । ऋतेन ऋतदेवेन दीर्घमत्यधिकं पृक्षोऽन्नमिषणंत । स्तोतार
> इच्छंति । ऋतेन ऋतदेवेन गावो धेनव ऋतं यज्ञं दक्षिणात्वेना विवेशुः ।
> प्रविशंति । यद्वा । गावो रश्मय ऋतमुदकमा विवेशुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

वपुषे (*vapuṣe*) means वपुष्मतः (*vapuṣmataḥ*) ("of the embodied"); the dative is used in the sense of the genitive. Firm (दृळ्हा (*dṛḷhā*) = दृढानि (*dṛḍhāni*)), sustaining (धरुणानि (*dharuṇāni*) = धारकाणि (*dhārakāṇi*)), many (पुरूणि (*purūṇi*) = बहूनि (*bahūni*)) and delightful (चंद्रा (*caṃdrā*) = *āhlādakāni*) are the forms (वपूंषि (*vapūṃṣi*) = रूपाणि (*rūpāṇi*)) belonging to Ṛta, the god Ṛta. Through Ṛta the singers greatly desire (इषणंत (*iṣaṇaṃta*)) food (पृक्षः (*pṛkṣaḥ*) = *annaṃ*). Through Ṛta the cows, the milch cows, enter the Ṛta, the sacrifice, in the form of the sacrificial fee (दक्षिणात्वेन (*dakṣiṇātvena*)). Or else: the cows, that is the rays, enter the Ṛta, that is water.

### Printed p. 129

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वपुषे (*vapuṣe*)—having an excellent form
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of the god Ṛta
- दृळ्हा (*dṛḷhā*)—firm
- धरुणानि (*dharuṇāni*)—having the power to sustain the world
- वपूंषि (*vapūṃṣi*)—forms
- पुरूणि (*purūṇi*)—of many kinds
- चंद्रा (*caṃdrā*)—and delightful
- संति (*saṃti*)—are
- ऋतेन (*ṛtena*)—with the help of the god Ṛta
- दीर्घं (*dīrghaṃ*)—abundant
- पृक्षः (*pṛkṣaḥ*)—food
- इषणंत (*iṣaṇaṃta*)—(the singers) desire (and obtain)
- ऋतेन (*ṛtena*)—through the god Ṛta alone
- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—milch cows
- ऋतं (*ṛtaṃ*)—the sacrifice
- आ विवेशुः (*ā viveśuḥ*)—enter

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The firm forms of the god Ṛta, of excellent form, which have the power to sustain the world, are of many kinds and delightful. With the help of Ṛta alone the singers obtain abundant food. With the help of Ṛta alone the milch cows enter the sacrifice.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Many are the stable, sustaining, delightful forms of the embodied Rita : by Rita are (the pious) expectant of food ; by Rita have the cows entered into the sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒तं ये॑मा॒न ऋ॒तमिद्व॑नोत्कृ॒तस्य॒ शुष्म॑स्तुर॒या उ॑ ग॒व्युः।  
> ऋ॒ताय॑ पृ॒थ्वी ब॑हु॒ले ग॑भी॒रे ऋ॒ताय॑ धे॒नू प॑र॒मे  ॑
> दुहाते ॥ १० ॥

> *ṛtaṃ yemāna ṛtamidvanotkṛtasya śuṣmasturayā u gavyuḥ|*  
> *ṛtāya pṛthvī bahule gabhīre ṛtāya dhenū parame*  
> *duhāte ‖ 10 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋतं । येमानः । ऋतं । इत् । वनोति । ऋतस्य । शुष्मः । तुर‍्ययाः । ऊं इति ।  
> गव्युः ।  
> ऋताय । पृथ्वी इति । बहुले इति । गभीरे इति । ऋताय । धेनू इति । परमे इति ।  
> दुहाते इति ॥ १० ॥

> *ṛtaṃ | yemānaḥ | ṛtaṃ | it | vanoti | ṛtasya | śuṣmaḥ | turyayāḥ | ūṃ iti |*  
> *gavyuḥ |*  
> *ṛtāya | pṛthvī iti | bahule iti | gabhīre iti | ṛtāya | dhenū iti | parame iti |*  
> *duhāte iti ‖ 10 ‖*

### Printed p. 130

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतमृतदेवं येमानो नियच्छन् स्तुतिभिर्वशीकुर्वन् स्तोता ऋतमित्
> ऋतदेवमेव वनोति । संभजते । ऋतस्य ऋतदेवस्य संबंधि शुष्मो बलं
> तुरयास्तूर्णं गव्युः । उ इति चार्थे । जलकामश्च भवति । बहुले विस्तीर्णे
> गभीरे दुरवगाहे पृथ्वी द्यावापृथिव्यावृताय ऋतदेवाय भवतः । धेनू
> प्रीणयित्र्यौ परमे उत्कृष्टे द्यावापृथिव्यावृताय ऋतदेवाय्कॆव दुहाते ।
> दोहनं कुर्वाते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The singer, restraining (येमानः (*yemānaḥ*) = नियच्छन् (*niyacchan*)) Ṛta, the god Ṛta, that is bringing him under control by praises, serves (वनोति (*vanoti*) = संभजते (*saṃbhajate*)) Ṛta alone. The strength (शुष्मः (*śuṣmaḥ*)) of Ṛta is swift (तुरयाः (*turayāḥ*) = तूर्णं (*tūrṇaṃ*)) and desirous (गव्युः (*gavyuḥ*)); उ (*u*) is in the sense of "and"; it is desirous of water. Heaven and earth, wide (बहुले (*bahule*) = विस्तीर्णे (*vistīrṇe*)) and deep (गभीरे (*gabhīre*) = दुरवगाहे (*duravagāhe*), hard to fathom), exist for Ṛta, the god Ṛta. Heaven and earth, the nourishing (धेनू (*dhenū*) = प्रीणयित्र्यौ (*prīṇayitryau*)) and supreme, excellent (परमे (*parame*) = उत्कृष्टे (*utkṛṣṭe*)) ones, milk (दुहाते (*duhāte*) = दोहनं कुर्वाते (*dohanaṃ kurvāte*)) for Ṛta, the god Ṛta, alone.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतं (*ṛtaṃ*)—the god Ṛta
- येमानः (*yemānaḥ*)—bringing under his own control (by praises)
- ऋतमित् (*ṛtamit*)—that very Ṛta
- वनोति (*vanoti*)—(the singer) serves
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of the god Ṛta
- शुष्कः (*śuṣkaḥ*)—the strength
- तुरयाः (*turayāḥ*)—which grows swiftly
- उ (*u*)—and
- गव्युः (*gavyuḥ*)—is also desirous of water
- बहुले (*bahule*)—wide
- गभीरे (*gabhīre*)—and exceedingly deep
- पृथ्वी (*pṛthvī*)—heaven and earth
- ऋताय (*ṛtāya*)—are ready to help the work of the god Ṛta
- धेनू (*dhenū*)—those that satisfy the world
- परमे (*parame*)—and are excellent, heaven and earth
- ऋताय (*ṛtāya*)—with the god Ṛta in view alone
- दुहाते (*duhāte*)—milk the water in the form of rain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The singer, bringing the god Ṛta under his control by his praise, serves him alone. The strength of the god Ṛta grows swiftly and is desirous of water. Heaven and earth, wide and exceedingly deep, are ready to help the work of Ṛta. Heaven and earth, which satisfy the world and are excellent, milk the water in the form of rain with Ṛta in view alone.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The (worshipper) subjecting Rita (to his will) verily enjoys Rita : the strength of Rita is (developed) with speed, and is desirous of (possesing) water : to Rita belong the wide and profound heaven and earth : supreme milch cows, they yield their milk to Rita.

### Printed p. 131
#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नू ष्टु॒त इं॑द्र॒ नू गृ॑णा॒न इषं॑ जरि॒त्रे न॒द्यो॒३॒॑३ न पी॑पेः ।  
> अका॑रि ते हरिवो॒ ब्रह्म॒ नव्यं॑ धि॒या स्या॑म र॒थ्यः॑ सदा॒साः ॥ ११ ॥

> *nū ṣṭuta iṃdra nū gṛṇāna iṣaṃ jaritre nadyo3 na pīpeḥ |*  
> *akāri te harivo brahma navyaṃ dhiyā syāma rathyaḥ sadāsāḥ ‖ 11 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नु । स्तुतः । इंद्र । नु । गृणानः । इषं । जरित्रे । नद्यः । न । पीपेरिति पीपेः ।  
> अकारि । ते । हरिवः । ब्रह्म । नव्यं । धिया । स्याम । रथ्यः । सदासाः ॥ ११ ॥

> *nu | stutaḥ | iṃdra | nu | gṛṇānaḥ | iṣaṃ | jaritre | nadyaḥ | na | pīperiti pīpeḥ |*  
> *akāri | te | harivaḥ | brahma | navyaṃ | dhiyā | syāma | rathyaḥ | sadāsāḥ ‖ 11 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र नु क्षिप्रं स्तुतः पूर्वैर्यषिभिः संस्तुतो नु क्षिप्रं गृणानोऽस्माभिः
> स्तूयमानस्त्वमिषमन्नं जरित्रे स्तोत्रे मह्यं नद्यो नाद्भिनदीरिव पीपेः ।
> प्यायय । प्रवृद्धं कुरु । हे हरिवो हरिसंज्ञ काश्वोपेतेंद्र ते तुभ्यं नव्यं
> नवतरं ब्रह्म स्तोत्रमकारि । अस्माभिः क्रियते । रथ्यो रथवंतो वयं धिया
> प्रज्ञारूपया स्तुत्या सदासास्त्वां सर्वदा भजमानास्त्वदर्थं हवीरूपस्यान्नस्य
> दातारो ना स्याम । भूयास्मै ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses Indra. "O Indra, quickly praised by the earlier seers, and quickly being praised by us, fill up (make abundant) food for me, your praiser, as rivers are filled with waters. O Hari-lord (Indra, owner of the horses named Hari), a newer hymn is made for you; it is made by us. May we, possessed of chariots, constantly serving you with praise in the form of insight, become givers of oblation-food for you." The closing word भूयास्मै (*bhūyāsmai*) is the "may we be" of the last clause.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- नु (*nu*)—exceedingly
- स्तुतः (*stutaḥ*)—one praised by the former seers
- नु (*nu*)—with the same devotion
- गृणानः (*gṛṇānaḥ*)—being one to be praised (by us also)
- नद्यः न (*nadyaḥ na*)—as rivers are filled with waters
- जरित्रे (*jaritre*)—to me, the praiser
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- पीपेः (*pīpeḥ*)—fill up and give
- हरिवः (*harivaḥ*)—O Indra, possessor of the horses called Hari
- ते (*te*)—addressed to you
- नव्यं (*navyaṃ*)—new
- ब्रह्म (*brahma*)—hymn
- अकारि (*akāri*)—is made
- रथ्यः (*rathyaḥ*)—we who possess chariots
- धिया (*dhiyā*)—with praise offered with understanding
- सदासाः (*sadāsāḥ*)—worshipping you and offering oblations
- स्याम (*syāma*)—grant that we may become

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who were exceedingly praised by the former seers and who, with the same devotion, are to be praised by us also, fill up food for me, your praiser, as rivers are filled with waters. O Indra, lord of the horses called Hari, a new hymn is made addressed to you.

### Printed p. 132
We who possess chariots, worshipping you with praise offered with understanding and offering oblations, may we become (givers of the oblation).

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Glorified (in the past), glorified, Indra, at present, satisfy your worshipper with food, as rivers (are filled with water): lord of horses, a new hymn has been made for you : may we, possessed of chariots, be ever diligent in your praise.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This Ṛk occurs not only in this sūkta but also as the final Ṛk of Sūktas 20–24.
इंद्र नु स्तुतः नु गृणानः नद्यः न जरित्रे इषं पीपेः (*iṃdra nu stutaḥ nu gṛṇānaḥ nadyaḥ na jaritre iṣaṃ pīpeḥ*)—O Indra, you were praised formerly by many seers. Now you are praised by us. As rivers are filled and flow fully with rain-water, fill up for us, who praise you, food (food and other sustenance); that is, give it as much as we wish.
नु (*nu*)—The particle नु (*nu*) has many meanings. In नु इत्येषोऽनेककर्मा । इदं नु करिष्यतीति हेत्वपदेशे । कथं नु करिष्यतीत्यनुपृष्टे (*nu ityeṣo 'nekakarmā | idaṃ nu kariṣyatīti hetvapadeśe | kathaṃ nu kariṣyatītyanupṛṣṭe*) (Ni. 1-4): when one says "he will do this, therefore" (इदं नु करिष्यति (*idaṃ nu kariṣyati*)) the word नु (*nu*) has the sense of cause, "therefore"; when one says कथं नु करिष्यति (*kathaṃ nu kariṣyati*) the word नु (*nu*) has the interrogative sense "how". Likewise अथाप्युपमार्थे भवति (*athāpyupamārthe bhavati*): the word नु (*nu*) is also used to indicate a comparison. For example:
अक्षो न चक्र्योः शूर बृहन्स्रु ते मह्ना रिरिचे रोदस्योः । वृक्षस्य नु ते पुरुहूत वया व्यू ३ तयो रुरुहुरिंद्र पूर्विः ॥ (*akṣo na cakryoḥ śūra bṛhansru te mahnā ririce rodasyoḥ | vṛkṣasya nu te puruhūta vayā vyū 3 tayo ruruhuriṃdra pūrviḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-24-3)
The author of the Nirukta cites this Ṛk (Ni. 1-4) and says that in वृक्षस्य नु ते (*vṛkṣasya nu te*) the word नु (*nu*) has the sense of comparison: वृक्षस्येव ते (*vṛkṣasyeva te*), "like a tree, or like the branches of a tree". Likewise:
नू मर्तो दयते सनिष्यन्यो विष्णव उरुगायाय दाशत् । (*nū marto dayate saniṣyanyo viṣṇava urugāyāya dāśat |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-100-11)
In Ṛks of this kind the word नु (*nu*) has the meaning क्षिप्रं (*kṣipraṃ*), "quickly, speedily"; and in
नू इंद्र शूर स्तवमान ऊती ब्रह्मजूतस्तन्वा वावृधस्व । (*nū iṃdra śūra stavamāna ūtī brahmajūtastanvā vāvṛdhasva |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-11)

### Printed p. 133
and similar Ṛks, the author of the Nirukta explains with examples that नु (*nu*) means "today, now, at once". In the present Ṛk the meaning is क्षिप्रं (*kṣipraṃ*), "quickly, speedily".
जरित्रे (*jaritre*)—The word जरितृ (*jaritṛ*) is read among the names of praisers (Ni. 3-19), and there is also the etymology जरिता गरिता (*jaritā garitā*) (Ni. 1-7); so जरित्रे (*jaritre*) here means "to me, who praise".
न (*na*)—The author of the Nirukta gives two kinds of meaning for the word न (*na*):
नेति प्रतिषेधार्थीयो भाषायां । उभयमन्वाधाय्यं । नेंद्रं देवममंसत इति प्रतिषेधार्थीयः पुरस्तादुपचारस्तस्य यत्पतिषेधति । दुर्मदासो न सुरायां इत्युपमार्थीयः । उपरिष्टादुपचारस्तस्य येनोपमिमीते ॥ (*neti pratiṣedhārthīyo bhāṣāyāṃ | ubhayamanvādhāyyaṃ | neṃdraṃ devamamaṃsata iti pratiṣedhārthīyaḥ purastādupacārastasya yatpatiṣedhati | durmadāso na surāyāṃ ityupamārthīyaḥ | upariṣṭādupacārastasya yenopamimīte ‖*) (Ni. 1-4)
That is, in the language of common usage, in modern Sanskrit that is not the Vedic language, the word न (*na*) is used in the sense of negation ("not, no"). This is known to all. But in the Vedas the word न (*na*) is used both in the sense of negation and in the sense of comparison. How? The word न (*na*) in the sentence नेंद्रं देवममंसत (*neṃdraṃ devamamaṃsata*) is used in the sense of negation. Negation (pratiṣedha) is not praising a person directly or to his face. In the sentence "Indra cannot be regarded as endowed with brilliance", the word "not" (the meaning of न (*na*)) indicates a sense contrary to the courteous statement concerning Indra. This is called the sense of negation. In the sentence दुर्मदासो न सुरायां (*durmadāso na surāyāṃ*) the word न (*na*) indicates comparison (likeness). The sentence means "arrogant like people who have drunk liquor". Here, speaking of the good or bad qualities of one person by comparing them with the good or bad qualities of another is the sense of comparison. Those who have drunk liquor, bereft of sense, behave in a base way out of arrogance; "arrogant like those people" is the sense of comparison. In the Vedas such meanings are indicated by the word न (*na*) and by the word इव (*iva*). The use of the word न (*na*) in this sense of comparison occurs only in the Vedas, not in the language in common use; there only the word इव (*iva*) is used instead.
In this Ṛk, which forms the conclusion of the sūkta, it is stated that the seer Vāmadeva, offering a newly composed hymn to the deity, prays for the deity's favour.

### Printed p. 134
## Sūkta 24 (Maṇḍala 4)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> का सुष्टुतिरित्येकादशर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं वामदेवस्यार्षमैंद्रं । दशम्यनुष्टुप् शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुभः । तथा चानुक्रमणिका । का सुष्टुतिरुपांत्यानुष्टुबिति ।
> विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning का सुष्टुतिः (*kā suṣṭutiḥ*) has eleven Ṛks and is the third sūkta. Its seer is Vāmadeva, its deity Indra. The tenth Ṛk is in the anuṣṭubh metre; the rest are triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇikā says: का सुष्टुतिरुपांत्यानुष्टुबिति (*kā suṣṭutirupāṃtyānuṣṭubiti*). The viniyoga (ritual application) is by indication (लैंगिकः (*laiṃgikaḥ*)).
**Anuvāda**: The sūkta का सुष्टुतिः (*kā suṣṭutiḥ*) is the third sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has eleven Ṛks. Vāmadeva is the seer and Indra the deity of this sūkta. The tenth Ṛk of this sūkta is in the anuṣṭubh metre; the remaining Ṛks are in triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: का सुष्टुतिरुपांत्यानुष्टुप् (*kā suṣṭutirupāṃtyānuṣṭup*). The viniyoga is लैंगिक (*laiṃgika*).
Sūkta 24; Maṇḍala 4; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 24. Aṣṭaka 3; Adhyāya 6; Vargas 11, 12. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 11. Ṛṣi: Vāmadeva. Devatā: Indra. Chandas: 1–9 and 11 triṣṭubh; 10 anuṣṭubh.
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> का सुष्टुतिः शवसः सूनुमिंद्रमर्वाचीनं राधस आ ववर्तत् ।  
> ददिर्हि वीरो गृणते वसूनि स गोपतिर्निष्पिधां नो  
> जनासः ॥ १ ॥

> *kā suṣṭutiḥ śavasaḥ sūnumiṃdramarvācīnaṃ rādhasa ā vavartat |*  
> *dadirhi vīro gṛṇate vasūni sa gopatirniṣpidhāṃ no*  
> *janāsaḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> का । सुस्तुतिः । शवसः । सूनुं । इंद्रं । अर्वाचीनं । राधसे । आ । ववर्तत् ।  
> ददिः । हि । वीरः । गृणते । वसूनि । सः । गोऽपतिः । निऽसिधां । नः । जनासः ॥ १ ॥

> *kā | sustutiḥ | śavasaḥ | sūnuṃ | iṃdraṃ | arvācīnaṃ | rādhase | ā | vavartat |*  
> *dadiḥ | hi | vīraḥ | gṛṇate | vasūni | saḥ | go 'patiḥ | ni 'sidhāṃ | naḥ | janāsaḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> का कीदृशी सुष्टुतिः शवसः सूनुं बलस्य पुत्रं । अतिबलिनमित्यर्थः । अर्वाचीनमस्मदभिमुखमिंद्रं परमैश्वर्ययुक्तं राधसे धनायास्मभ्यं धनं दातुमा

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"What (of what kind) is the good praise (that can turn) Indra, the son of strength, that is, the very mighty one, facing us (अर्वाचीनं (*arvācīnaṃ*)), Indra of supreme lordship, so as to give wealth to us, for riches (राधसे (*rādhase*))? Let it turn him (आ ववर्तत् (*ā vavartat*), आवर्तयेत् (*āvartayet*))." The sentence is continued in the next block.

### Printed p. 135

> ववर्तत् । आवर्तयेत् । हे जनासो जना यजमाना वीरो विक्रांतो गोपतिः
> पश्वादिधनस्य पालकः स इंद्रो निष्पिधां निष्टेद्धृणां शत्रूणां संबंधीनि वसूनि
> धनानि गृणते गृणद्भ्यः स्तोतृभ्यो नोऽस्मभ्यं ददिर्हि । दाता खलु भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O people (जनासः (*janāsaḥ*)), sacrificers: Indra, the hero (valiant), lord of cows, protector of wealth such as cattle, is a giver (ददिर्हि (*dadirhi*), दाता खलु भवति (*dātā khalu bhavati*)) to us, the praisers, of the riches belonging to the enemies (निष्पिधां (*niṣpidhāṃ*), glossed निष्टेद्धृणां शत्रूणां (*niṣṭeddhṛṇāṃ śatrūṇāṃ*))." **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- का (*kā*)—what kind of
- सुष्टुतिः (*suṣṭutiḥ*)—excellent praise
- शवसः सूनुं (*śavasaḥ sūnuṃ*)—the son of strength (the very mighty)
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- राधसे (*rādhase*)—to give us wealth
- अर्वाचीनं (*arvācīnaṃ*)—so that he is turned towards us
- आ ववर्तत् (*ā vavartat*)—can it turn him?
- जनासः (*janāsaḥ*)—O sacrificers who are men
- वीरः (*vīraḥ*)—the valiant
- गोपतिः (*gopatiḥ*)—protector of wealth such as cattle
- सः (*saḥ*)—that Indra
- निष्पिधां (*niṣpidhāṃ*)—belonging to the enemies who obstruct
- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—riches
- गृणते (*gṛṇate*)—to us who praise him
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- ददिर्हि (*dadirhi*)—is the giver

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

What kind of praise can turn Indra, who is able to give us wealth and the like and is exceedingly mighty, towards us and make him favour us? O sacrificers who are men, Indra, the valiant protector of wealth such as cattle, snatches the riches belonging to the hostile enemies and is the giver of them to us who praise him.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
What suitable praise may bring the son of strength, Indra, before us, to give us wealth : the hero, the lord of cattle, is the donor, oh man, of the wealth of his adversaries to him who glorifies him.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शवसः सूनुं (*śavasaḥ sūnuṃ*)—Words such as शवसः सूनुं (*śavasaḥ sūnuṃ*) and सहसः पुत्र (*sahasaḥ putra*) are used in the Ṛgveda with reference to Agni. Because in churning fire one must churn by applying force, Agni is customarily addressed as the son of strength (born of the application of force). In the present Ṛk these words are used with reference to Indra. As the commentator says, अतिबलिनमित्यर्थः (*atibalinamityarthaḥ*): the words are to be understood as used in the sense "Indra, who is exceedingly strong".
सः निष्पिधां वसूनि गृणते नः ददिर्हि (*saḥ niṣpidhāṃ vasūni gṛṇate naḥ dadirhi*)—It is said that that Indra gives us the riches belonging to the enemies who oppose us. That is, when we fight with enemies

### Printed p. 136
victory comes to us by Indra's favour and the riches that the enemies hold fall to us; this is the intention indicated. **NOTE**
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स वृत्रहत्ये हव्यः स ईड्यः स सुष्टुत इंद्रः सत्यराधाः ।  
> स यामन्ना मघवा मर्त्याय ब्रह्मण्यते सुस्वये वरिवो  
> धात् ॥ २ ॥

> *sa vṛtrahatye havyaḥ sa īḍyaḥ sa suṣṭuta iṃdraḥ satyarādhāḥ |*  
> *sa yāmannā maghavā martyāya brahmaṇyate susvaye varivo*  
> *dhāt ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । वृत्रहत्ये । हव्यः । सः । ईड्यः । सः । सुस्तुतः । इंद्रः । सत्यराधाः ।  
> सः । यामन् । आ । मघवा । मर्त्याय । ब्रह्मण्यते । सुस्वये । वरिवः । धात् ॥

> *saḥ | vṛtrahatye | havyaḥ | saḥ | īḍyaḥ | saḥ | sustutaḥ | iṃdraḥ | satyarādhāḥ |*  
> *saḥ | yāman | ā | maghavā | martyāya | brahmaṇyate | susvaye | varivaḥ | dhāt ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रो वृत्रहत्ये वृत्राणां शत्रूणां हत्ये हनने निमित्तभूते सति
> यामन्युद्धे हव्य आह्वातव्यो भवत्ति । स इंद्र ईड्यः स्तुत्यो भवति । स इंद्रः
> सुष्टुतः सन् सत्यराधा यजमानेभ्यो दातुं सत्यधनो भवति । मघवा धनवान् स
> इंद्रो ब्रह्मण्यते स्तोत्रमिच्छते सुस्वये सोमं सुन्वते मर्त्याय मनुष्याय
> यजमानाय वरिवो धनं । वरिव इति धननामैतत् । आ धात् । आ समंताद्ददाति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra, in वृत्रहत्ये (*vṛtrahatye*), that is, when there is occasion for the slaying of the Vṛtras (enemies), in battle (यामन् (*yāman*)), becomes one to be invoked (हव्यः (*havyaḥ*)). That Indra is to be praised (ईड्यः (*īḍyaḥ*)). That Indra, being well praised, becomes सत्यराधाः (*satyarādhāḥ*), one of true wealth for giving to the sacrificers. मघवा (*maghavā*) is "wealthy". That Indra gives fully (आ धात् (*ā dhāt*), आ समंताद्ददाति (*ā samaṃtāddadāti*)) वरिवः (*varivaḥ*), wealth, to the mortal, the man, the sacrificer, who desires a hymn (ब्रह्मण्यते (*brahmaṇyate*)) and who presses soma (सुस्वये (*susvaye*)). Sāyaṇa says: वरिव इति धननामैतत् (*variva iti dhananāmaitat*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- वृत्रहत्ये (*vṛtrahatye*)—in the enemy-destroying
- यामन् (*yāman*)—time of battle
- हव्यः (*havyaḥ*)—is one called for help
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- ईड्यः (*īḍyaḥ*)—is worthy of worship
- सः इंद्रः (*saḥ iṃdraḥ*)—that same Indra who is worthy of worship
- सुष्टुतः (*suṣṭutaḥ*)—when exceedingly praised
- सत्यराधाः (*satyarādhāḥ*)—becomes a giver of wealth that is real (satyātmaka) **NOTE**
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—wealthy
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same Indra
- ब्रह्मण्यते (*brahmaṇyate*)—to the one who praises
- सुष्टये (*suṣṭaye*)—and presses soma **NOTE**
- मर्त्याय (*martyāya*)—to the sacrificer
- वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)—wealth
- आ धात् (*ā dhāt*)—gives fully

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The valiant Indra is called for help in battle, which destroys enemies. He is exceedingly worthy of worship. Indra, thus worthy of worship, when praised by the sacrificers, becomes a giver of real (satyātmaka)

### Printed p. 137
**Bhāvārtha** (continued)
…he becomes the giver of wealth. That same wealthy Indra gives wealth in full to the sacrificer, a mortal, who praises him and presses Soma for the offering.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
He, Indra, is to be invoked for the destruction of Vritra : he, the deservedly praised. is to be worshipped : the real donor of wealth, he, Maghavan, gives wealth, (acquired) in battle, to the mortal who offers him prayer and libations.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Since Indra's favour is necessary for victory in battle and the like, we call upon Indra at that time for the gain of victory. By praising Indra we please him, and he gives us wealth and other things. The idea suggested here is that Indra gives wealth and the like, and so gladdens the sacrificers who offer praise and who press the Soma juice and offer it to Indra.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तमिन्नरो॒ वि ह्व॑यंते समी॒के रि॑रि॒क्वांस॑स्त॒न्वः॑ कृण्वत॒ त्रां ।  
> मि॒थो यत्या॒गमु॒भया॑सो॒ अग्म॒न्नर॑स्तो॒कस्य॒ तन॑यस्य  ॒
> सात् ॥ ३ ॥

> *taminnaro vi hvayaṃte samīke ririkvāṃsastanvaḥ kṛṇvata trāṃ |*  
> *mitho yatyāgamubhayāso agmannarastokasya tanayasya*  
> *sāt ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तं । इत् । नरः । वि । ह्वयंते । संईके । रिरिक्वांसः । तन्नः । कृण्वत । त्रां ।  
> मिथः । यत् । त्यागं । उभयासः । अग्मन् । नरः । तोकस्य । तनयस्य । सातौ ॥ ३ ॥

> *taṃ | it | naraḥ | vi | hvayaṃte | saṃīke | ririkvāṃsaḥ | tannaḥ | kṛṇvata | trāṃ |*  
> *mithaḥ | yat | tyāgaṃ | ubhayāsaḥ | agman | naraḥ | tokasya | tanayasya | sātau ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नरो नेतारो मनुष्याः समीके युद्धे तमित्तमेवेंद्रं वि ह्वयंते ।
> विविधमाह्वानं कुर्वते । तन्नः स्वकीयानि शरीराणि रिरिकांसस्तपसा रेचयंतो
> 138
> यजमानास्तमेवेंद्रं त्रां त्रातारं कृण्वत । कृण्वते । कुर्वते । यद्यदा मिथः
> परस्परं संगता उभयास उभये यजमानाः स्तोतारश्च त्यागं त्यागकर्तारं
> दातारमिंद्रमग्मन् उपगच्छंति तदा नर उभयविधा मनुष्यास्तोकस्य पुत्रस्य
> तनयस्य पौत्रस्य च सात्लाभे निमित्तभूते सति तमेवेंद्रं त्रातारं कुर्वंतीति
> संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The men (the leaders, human beings) call upon that very Indra in various ways in the battle (समीके (*samīke*) = in battle). The sacrificers, रिरिक्वांसः (*ririkvāṃsaḥ*), who make their own bodies lean (empty them) by austerity, make that same Indra their protector (त्रां (*trāṃ*) = protector; कृण्वत (*kṛṇvata*) = कृण्वते (*kṛṇvate*), they make). When both parties, the sacrificers and the praisers, come together and approach Indra, the renouncer (त्यागं (*tyāgaṃ*) = the giver), then both kinds of men make that same Indra their protector, there being occasion for obtaining (सात् (*sāt*) = gain) a son (तोकस्य (*tokasya*)) and a grandson (तनयस्य (*tanayasya*)). This is the construction.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नरः (*naraḥ*)—men
- सविाकॆ (*saviākè*)—in battle
- तमित् (*tamit*)—that same Indra (Indra alone)
- वि ह्वयंते (*vi hvayaṃte*)—call upon in various ways, praising
- तन्नः (*tannaḥ*)—their bodies
- रिरिक्वांसः (*ririkvāṃsaḥ*)—the sacrificers who make themselves lean through austerity
- त्रां (*trāṃ*)—(Indra alone) as their protector
- कृण्वते (*kṛṇvate*)—makes (for themselves) **NOTE**
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- उभयासः (*ubhayāsaḥ*)—the sacrificers and the praisers
- मिथः (*mithaḥ*)—gathering together
- त्यागं (*tyāgaṃ*)—Indra, the giver
- अग्मन् (*agman*)—approach, then
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—both groups
- तोकस्य (*tokasya*)—of what takes the form of a son
- तनयस्य (*tanayasya*)—and of what takes the form of a grandson, the wealth
- सात् (*sāt*)—for the sake of obtaining (they choose Indra)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificers, being men, call upon Indra alone in battle for help, praising him in various ways. The sacrificers who worship Indra after making their bodies lean by austerity make him alone their protector. When the sacrificers and the praisers gather together and approach the bountiful Indra, there is no doubt that both groups obtain wealth in the form of sons and grandsons.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Men verily call upon him in battle : the (devout) inflictors of auste- rity upon their persons constitute him their preserver : when both (the worshipper and the priest) approach together the bountiful Indra, men (succeed) in (obtaining) the gift of sons and grandsons.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क्र॒तू॒यंति॑ क्षि॒तयो॒ योग॑ उग्राशुषा॒णासो॑ मि॒थो  ॑
> अर्णसात् ।

> *kratūyaṃti kṣitayo yoga ugrāśuṣāṇāso mitho*  
> *arṇasāt |*

### Printed p. 139

> सं यद्विशोऽववृत्रंत युध्मा आदिन्नेम इंद्रयंते  
> अभीकेः ॥ ४ ॥

> *saṃ yadviśo 'vavṛtraṃta yudhmā ādinnema iṃdrayaṃte*  
> *abhīkeḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> क्रतुयंति । क्षितयः । योगे । उग्र । आशुषाणासः । मिथः । अर्णसात् ।  
> सं । यत् । विशः । अववृत्रंत । युध्माः । आत् । इत् । नेमे । इंद्रयंते ।  
> अभीके ॥ ४ ॥

> *kratuyaṃti | kṣitayaḥ | yoge | ugra | āśuṣāṇāsaḥ | mithaḥ | arṇasāt |*  
> *saṃ | yat | viśaḥ | avavṛtraṃta | yudhmāḥ | āt | it | neme | iṃdrayaṃte |*  
> *abhīke ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उग्रोद्गूर्णबलेंद्र आशुषाणासो व्याप्नुवंतः क्षितयो मनुष्याः ।
> क्षितय इति मनुष्यनामैतत् । अर्णसात् सस्यादिफलसिद्ध्यर्थमुदकलाभे निमित्ते सति
> मिथः परस्परं योगे संबंधे सति क्रतूयंति । क्रतूनि कर्माणीच्छंति । यद्यदा
> युध्मा योधयित्यो विशः प्रजा अभीके युद्धे । अभीक इति संग्रामनामैतत् ।
> समववृत्रंत संवर्तंते परस्परं संगच्छंते आदित् तदानीमेव नेमे केचन
> भाग्यवंतो योधका इंद्रयंते । युद्ध इंद्रमिच्छंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O fierce Indra, of upraised strength! Men, क्षितयः (*kṣitayaḥ*) (क्षितय इति मनुष्यनामैतत् (*kṣitaya iti manuṣyanāmaitat*)), pervading (आशुषाणासः (*āśuṣāṇāsaḥ*)), when there is occasion to obtain water for the perfecting of the fruit of crops and the like (अर्णसात् (*arṇasāt*)), and there is mutual connection (योगे (*yoge*)), desire rites (क्रतूयंति (*kratūyaṃti*) = they desire क्रतूनि (*kratūni*), rites). When the combative peoples, the warriors, assemble in battle (अभीक इति संग्रामनामैतत् (*abhīka iti saṃgrāmanāmaitat*)), meeting one another (समववृत्रंत (*samavavṛtraṃta*)), then at that very moment some fortunate fighters desire Indra in the battle (इंद्रयंते (*iṃdrayaṃte*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उग्र (*ugra*)—O Indra, possessed of manifested might
- आशुषाणासः (*āśuṣāṇāsaḥ*)—eager for rain
- क्षितयः (*kṣitayaḥ*)—men
- अर्णसात् (*arṇasāt*)—for the sake of rain
- मिथः (*mithaḥ*)—together
- योगे (*yoge*)—joining
- ऋतूयंति (*ṛtūyaṃti*)—wish to perform rites **NOTE**
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- युध्माः (*yudhmāḥ*)—desirous of battle
- विशः (*viśaḥ*)—the people
- अभीके (*abhīke*)—in battle
- सं अववृत्रंत (*saṃ avavṛtraṃta*)—gather together
- आदित् (*ādit*)—then
- नेमे (*neme*)—only some men of merit
- इंद्रयंते (*iṃdrayaṃte*)—desire you, the lord, for help

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, possessed of manifested and terrible might: men eager for rain join together for the sake of rain and wish to perform rites. When people desirous of battle gather together for fighting, then only some meritorious ones desire you, the lord, and call upon you for help.

### Printed p. 140

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Powerful Indra, men variously dispersed, come mutually together to celebrate sacred rites for the sake of obtaining rain: when men who are combatants assemble in battle, there are some of them who rely upon Indra.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आदिद्द॒ नेम॑ इंद्रि॒यं य॑जंत॒ आदित्प॒क्तिः पु॑रो॒ळापं॑ रिरिच्यात् ।  
> आदित्सोमो॒ वि प॑पृच्या॒दसु॑ष्पी॒नादिज्जु॑ जोष वृष॒भं यज॑ध्यै ॥

> *ādidda nema iṃdriyaṃ yajaṃta āditpaktiḥ puroḷāpaṃ riricyāt |*  
> *āditsomo vi papṛcyādasuṣpīnādijju joṣa vṛṣabhaṃ yajadhyai ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आत् । इत् । ह । नेमे । इंद्रियं । यजंते । आत् । इत् । पक्तिः । पुरोळाशं ।  
> रिरिच्यात् ।  
> आत् । इत् । सोमः । वि । पपृच्यात् । असुस्वीन् । आत् । इत् । जुजोष । वृषभं ।  
> यजध्यै ॥ ५ ॥

> *āt | it | ha | neme | iṃdriyaṃ | yajaṃte | āt | it | paktiḥ | puroḷāśaṃ |*  
> *riricyāt |*  
> *āt | it | somaḥ | vi | papṛcyāt | asusvīn | āt | it | jujoṣa | vṛṣabhaṃ |*  
> *yajadhyai ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आदित्तदानीमेव युद्धकाले नेमेऽन्ये योधा इंद्रियं बलवंतमिंद्रं
> यजंते ह । पूजयंति खलु । कश्चिदादिदनंतरमेव पुरोळाशं पिष्टरूपं हविः
> पक्तिः पचन् रिरिच्यात् । इंद्राय दद्यात् । आदिदनंतरमेव सोमोऽभिषुतसोमो
> यजमानोऽसुष्वीननभिषुतसोमान्यजर्मानान्वि पपृच्यात् । धनैः पृथक्कुर्यात् ।
> आदिदनंतरमेव कश्चिद्वृषभं कामानां वर्षितारमिंद्रं यजध्यै यष्टुं जुजोष।
> सेवते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Then, at that very time of battle, other warriors (नेमे (*neme*) = others) worship (यजंते ह (*yajaṃte ha*) = they indeed honour) the mighty Indra (इंद्रियं (*iṃdriyaṃ*)). Immediately after, someone, cooking (पक्तिः (*paktiḥ*) = cooking) the purodāśa, an oblation in the form of a cake of ground meal, would offer (रिरिच्यात् (*riricyāt*)) it to Indra. Immediately after, the sacrificer who has pressed Soma would separate by gifts of wealth (पपृच्यात् (*papṛcyāt*)) those sacrificers who have not pressed Soma (असुष्वीन् (*asuṣvīn*)). Immediately after, someone is eager (जुजोष (*jujoṣa*) = serves) to worship (यजध्यै (*yajadhyai*)) the bull (वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)), Indra the showerer of desires.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आदित् (*ādit*)—as soon as the battle has come
- नेमे (*neme*)—some warriors
- इंद्रियं (*iṃdriyaṃ*)—the mighty Indra
- यजंते ह (*yajaṃte ha*)—worship
- आदित् (*ādit*)—immediately thereupon
- पुरोळाशं (*puroḷāśaṃ*)—the oblation in the form of purodāśa
- पक्तिः (*paktiḥ*)—cooking
- रिरिच्यात् (*riricyāt*)—they offer to Indra
### Printed p. 141
- आदित् (*ādit*)—immediately
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—the sacrificer who has pressed the Soma
- असुष्वीन् (*asuṣvīn*)—(not being a sacrificer, hence) men who do not press Soma
- पपृच्यात् (*papṛcyāt*)—he separates from himself
- आदित् (*ādit*)—at once
- वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)—addressing Indra, the showerer of desires
- यजध्यै (*yajadhyai*)—to perform the sacrifice
- जुजोष (*jujoṣa*)—desires

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as the battle has come, some warriors worship the mighty Indra. Immediately thereupon they cook the oblation in the form of purodāśa and offer it to Indra. Immediately the sacrificer who has pressed Soma for the sacrifice separates himself from the man who is not a sacrificer (and hence has not pressed Soma). At once he desires to perform sacrifice to Indra, the showerer of desires.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Thereupon some verily worship the powerful (Indra); thereupon one man prepares the buttered cake that he may offer it to (Indra); thereupon the offerer of the Soma he distinguishes from him who presents no libation ; thereupon some one rejoices to worship the showerer (of benefits).

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कृ॒णोत्य॑स्मै॒ वरि॑वो॒ य इ॒त्थेंद्रा॑य॒ सोम॑मुश॒ते सु॒नोति॑ ।  
> स॒ध्री॒चीने॑न॒ मन॒सावि॑वेनं॒ तमित्सखा॑यं कृणुते स॒मत्सु॑ ॥

> *kṛṇotyasmai varivo ya ittheṃdrāya somamuśate sunoti |*  
> *sadhrīcīnena manasāvivenaṃ tamitsakhāyaṃ kṛṇute samatsu ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कृणोति । अस्मै । वरिवः । यः । इत्था । इंद्राय । सोमं । उशते । सुनोति ।  
> सध्रीचीनेन । मनसा । अविवेनं । तं । इत् । सखायं । कृणुते । समत्सु ॥ ६ ॥

> *kṛṇoti | asmai | varivaḥ | yaḥ | itthā | iṃdrāya | somaṃ | uśate | sunoti |*  
> *sadhrīcīnena | manasā | avivenaṃ | taṃ | it | sakhāyaṃ | kṛṇute | samatsu ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो यजमानः सोममुशते कामयमानायेत्थामुत्र स्वर्गलोके
> स्थितायेंद्राय सुनोति अभिषवं करोति इंद्रोऽप्यस्मै यजमानाय वरिवो

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificer who presses Soma, that is, performs the pressing, for Indra, who desires it (उशते (*uśate*) = desiring) and who is thus (इत्था (*itthā*)) established yonder in the world of heaven: Indra too makes for that sacrificer wealth (वरिवः (*varivaḥ*))…

### Printed p. 142

> धनं कृणोति । करोति । सध्रीचीनेन संगतेन मनसा प्रवणेन चित्तेनाविवेनं ।
> वेनतिः कांतिकर्मा । विवेनो विगतेच्छः । तदन्योऽविवेनः । आत्मानं कामयमानं
> तमित्तमेव सुन्वंतं यजमानं सखायं मित्रं समत्सु संग्रामेष्विंद्रः कृणुते ।
> करोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

…(कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*) = he makes). With a concordant (सध्रीचीनेन (*sadhrīcīnena*) = joined) mind, one inclined (to him), अविवेनं (*avivenaṃ*): वेनतिः कांतिकर्मा (*venatiḥ kāṃtikarmā*); विवेनो विगतेच्छः (*viveno vigatecchaḥ*), one whose desire is gone; the other than that is अविवेनं (*avivenaṃ*). That very sacrificer, who presses Soma, desiring himself (Indra), Indra makes (कृणुते (*kṛṇute*)) his friend (सखायं (*sakhāyaṃ*)) in the battles (समत्सु (*samatsu*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma
- उशते (*uśate*)—for him who desires
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—addressing Indra
- सुनोति (*sunoti*)—presses the Soma
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—for such a sacrificer
- इत्था (*itthā*)—even though (Indra) is in heaven
- वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)—wealth
- कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*)—provides
- सध्रीचीनेन (*sadhrīcīnena*)—with a mind united to himself in the form of yoga
- मनसा (*manasā*)—with mind
- अविवेनं (*avivenaṃ*)—one who has meditated on himself and desired him
- तमित् (*tamit*)—that sacrificer alone
- सखायं (*sakhāyaṃ*)—as a friend
- समत्तु (*samattu*)—in battles **NOTE**
- कृणुते (*kṛṇute*)—makes for himself

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificer on earth who presses Soma for offering to Indra, who desires Soma: to such a sacrificer Indra, though he is in heaven, provides wealth from there itself. Indra makes in battles his friend that sacrificer who, with a mind united to him in the form of yoga, has meditated on him alone and desired his help.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra bestows wealth upon him who offers a libation to him, desiring it, though in another sphere; and with an humble mind, makes him who is devoted to him his friend in combats.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य इंद्रा॑य सु॒नव॒त्सोम॑म॒द्य पचा॑त्प॒क्तीरु॒त भृ॒ज्जाति॑  ॒
> धानाः ।  
> प्रति॑ मना॒योरु॒चथा॑नि॒ हर्य॒न्तस्मि॑न्दध॒द्वृष॑णं॒ शुष्म॒मिंद्रः॑ ॥

> *ya iṃdrāya sunavatsomamadya pacātpaktīruta bhṛjjāti*  
> *dhānāḥ |*  
> *prati manāyorucathāni haryantasmindadhadvṛṣaṇaṃ śuṣmamiṃdraḥ ‖*

### Printed p. 143

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । इंद्राय । सुनवत् । सोमं । अद्य । पचात् । पक्तिः । उत । भृज्जाति । धानाः।  
> प्रति । मनायोः । उचथानि । हर्यन् । तस्मिन् । दधत् । वृषणं । शुष्मं । इंद्रः ॥७॥

> *yaḥ | iṃdrāya | sunavat | somaṃ | adya | pacāt | paktiḥ | uta | bhṛjjāti | dhānāḥ|*  
> *prati | manāyoḥ | ucathāni | haryan | tasmin | dadhat | vṛṣaṇaṃ | śuṣmaṃ | iṃdraḥ ‖7‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अद्यास्मिन्दिवसे यो यजमान इंद्राय परमैश्वर्ययुक्ताय देवाय
> सोमं सोमलक्षणमन्नं सुनवत् सुनुयात् अभिषवं कुर्यात् । तथा यः
> पक्तीः पक्तव्यांश्च पुरोडाशादीन्पचात् पचेत् पचनं कुर्यात् । उतापि च यो
> धाना भर्जनयोग्यान्यवान् । अत्र बृष्टयववाची धानाशब्दस्तद्योग्यं यवमात्रं
> लक्षयति । भृज्जाति भृज्जेत् भर्जनं कुर्यात् । इंद्रो मनायोः स्तुतिमिच्छतो
> यजमानस्योचथानि स्तोत्राणि प्रति हर्यन् प्रतिकामयमानः सन् तस्मिन्यजमाने
> वृषणं कामानां वर्षणसमर्थं शुष्मं बलं दधत् । धारयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Today, on this day, the sacrificer who would press (सुनवत् (*sunavat*) = would perform the pressing of) Soma, the food characterised as Soma, for Indra, the god endowed with supreme lordship; and who would cook (पचात् (*pacāt*)) the cooked things (पक्तीः (*paktīḥ*)), the purodāśas and other things to be cooked; and also who would parch (भृज्जाति (*bhṛjjāti*)) the grains (धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)), barley fit for parching — here the word धाना (*dhānā*), denoting parched barley, indicates barley only, that which is fit for it. Indra, desiring (हर्यन् (*haryan*) = desiring in return) the praises (उचथानि (*ucathāni*) = hymns) of the sacrificer who longs for praise (मनायोः (*manāyoḥ*)), bestows (दधत् (*dadhat*) = holds) upon that sacrificer bull-like strength (शुष्मं (*śuṣmaṃ*)), capable of showering desires.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्य (*adya*)—today
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—addressing Indra
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma
- सुनवत् (*sunavat*)—would press
- पक्तिः (*paktiḥ*)—the purodāśas and the like fit for cooking
- पचात् (*pacāt*)—would cook
- उत (*uta*)—and
- धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)—grains such as barley
- भृज्जाति (*bhṛjjāti*)—would parch, making them fit for eating (such a one)
- मनायोः (*manāyoḥ*)—of the sacrificer who is a praiser
- उचथानि (*ucathāni*)—the hymns
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- प्रति हर्यन् (*prati haryan*)—desiring and accepting
- तस्मिन् (*tasmin*)—in that sacrificer
- वृषणं (*vṛṣaṇaṃ*)—showering desires
- शुष्मं (*śuṣmaṃ*)—strength
- दधत् (*dadhat*)—brings about

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificer who praises Indra, and who today, addressing Indra, presses Soma, cooks the purodāśas and other things fit for cooking, parches barley and other grains to make them fit for eating, and offers them to Indra with praise of him: Indra, desiring and accepting the hymns of such a sacrificer, brings about in him strength that showers desires.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra accepting graciously the praises of his devoted (worshipper), who to-day pours out the libation to him, who toasts the buttered cakes

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य॒दा स॑म॒र्यं व्यचे॒दृघा॑वा दी॒र्घं यदा॒जिम॒भ्यख्य॑द॒र्यः।  
> अचि॑क्रद॒द्वृष॑णं॒ पत्न्यच्छा॑ दुरो॒ण आ निशि॑तं सोम॒सुद्भिः॑ ॥८॥

> *yadā samaryaṃ vyacedṛghāvā dīrghaṃ yadājimabhyakhyadaryaḥ|*  
> *acikradadvṛṣaṇaṃ patnyacchā duroṇa ā niśitaṃ somasudbhiḥ ‖8‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यदा । समर्यं । वि । अचेत् । यघावा । दीर्घं । यत् । आजिं । अभि । अख्यत्।  
> अर्यः ।  
> अचिक्रदत् । वृषणं । पत्नि । अच्छ । दुरोणे । आ । निशितं । सोमसुत्भिः ॥ ८ ॥

> *yadā | samaryaṃ | vi | acet | yaghāvā | dīrghaṃ | yat | ājiṃ | abhi | akhyat|*  
> *aryaḥ |*  
> *acikradat | vṛṣaṇaṃ | patni | accha | duroṇe | ā | niśitaṃ | somasutbhiḥ ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यदा ऋघावा शत्रूणां हिंसक इंद्रः समर्यं सह मर्तव्यं शत्रुं
> व्यचेत् व्यज्ञासीत् विशेषेण जानीयात् । यद्यदार्यः स्वामींद्रो दीर्घमत्यधिकमाजिं संग्राममभ्यख्यत् अभिपश्येत् तदा तस्येंद्रस्य पत्नि दुरोणे यज्ञगृहे
> सोमसुद्भिः सोमं सुन्वद्भिर्युत्विग्निर्निशितं तीक्ष्णीकृतं । सोमपानेनोत्साहवंतमित्यर्थः । वृषणं कामानां वर्षितारमिंद्रमच्छाभि अचिक्रदत् । आक्रंदति ।
> आह्वयते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When Indra, the destroyer of enemies (ṛghāvā), distinguishes the enemy who is to be slain together with him (samaryam), and when the lord (arya) Indra looks upon the long, extended battle, then his wife, in the sacrificial house (duroṇa), calls out to Indra, the showerer of desires (vṛṣaṇam), who has been sharpened (niśitam) by the priests pressing Soma, that is, made eager by drinking Soma. "Acikradat" means "calls, summons".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यदा (*yadā*)—when
- ऋघावा (*ṛghāvā*)—Indra, the destroyer of enemies
- समर‍्यं (*samaryaṃ*)—the enemy fit to be slain at once
- व्यचेत् (*vyacet*)—has well understood
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—Indra, the lord
- दीर्घं (*dīrghaṃ*)—exceedingly
- आजिं (*ājiṃ*)—in battle
- अभ्यख्यत् (*abhyakhyat*)—looks on with special interest, then
- पत्नि (*patni*)—his wife
- दुरोणे (*duroṇe*)—in the sacrificial house
- सोमसुद्भिः (*somasudbhiḥ*)—by the priests who press Soma
- निशितं (*niśitaṃ*)—one made eager
- वृषणं (*vṛṣaṇaṃ*)—and showerer of desires, Indra
- अच्छ (*accha*)—so that he comes towards her
- आ चक्रदत् (*ā cakradat*)—calls out and summons

### Printed p. 145

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When Indra, the destroyer of enemies, has well understood the enemy fit to be slain at once, and when Indra the lord stands looking on the battle with great eagerness, then his wife, in the sacrificial house, calls out to Indra, who has been made eager by the Soma-pressing priests and who is the showerer of desires, so that he may come towards her.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
When the destroyer (of enemies) distinguishes a mortal foe ; when the lord is engaged in the long (continued) battle, (his) bride summons to the dwelling the showerer (of benefits), encompassed by the offerers of the libation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The explanation of this Ṛk is somewhat difficult. The commentator says that arya means the lord Indra and patni means Indra's wife Indrāṇī. His main sense is: while Indra is engaged in a long battle on behalf of those who praise him, Indrāṇī, his wife, summons the far-off, battle-absorbed Indra to come, in order to grant their wishes to the priests and sacrificers who are offering Soma-juice for Indra. But some Western scholars take arya to mean the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice: since that sacrificer is engaged in battle and does not come for a long time, his wife has the Soma-juice prepared by the priests and invokes Indra on behalf of the sacrificer (her husband). This is the sense they think the Ṛk suggests. The English scholar Wilson has expressed the opinion: "Sayana explains arya by svāmi, lord, that is Indra ; and patni in the following passage, he says is Indra's wife; but it would be more consistent with the concluding passage to render arya as the name of the orthodox Hindu, in whose behalf his wife propitiates Indra."

> भवेत् । आध मूल्यार्थमेतत्स्वाद्विजार्यव तु निर्णयः ॥ इत्येवं समयोऽकारि

> इममित्यचि ॥ अतश्च दृच एकार्घो भूयसा वस्नमित्ययमिति ॥ अयमर्थः
> प्रतिपाद्यते । कश्चिद्धूयसा भूम्ना पण्येन द्रव्येण कनीयोऽल्पतरं वस्नं वसु
> मूल्यं धनमचरत् । प्राप्नोति । पुर्यन् क्रेतारं पुनर्गच्छन् स विक्रेताय.
> मर्थो मयाविक्रीत इति ब्रुवन्न कानिषं । मूल्यपूर्तिं कामयते ॥ व्यत्ययेनोत्तमपुरुषः ॥ स विक्रेता भूयसा धनेन कनीयोऽल्पतरं मूल्यं नारिरेचीत् ।
> क्रेतुः सकाशान्न रिक्तीकरोति । न लभत इत्यर्थः । दीना असमर्था दक्षाः समर्थाश्च
> वाणं वचनं वि प्रदुहंति । यथोक्तं क्रयकाले तथैव लभंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

On this Ṛk, traditional earlier teachers recite certain verses, which are written out here. In sum: a man who takes a small price for goods worth much, and then goes back to the buyer saying "it was not sold by me", desires the completion of the price; but the seller would not make up the price with a larger sum; one who has sold at a lower price does not afterwards get more; what was agreed at the time governs both, not otherwise. If the agreement was "this is only a sale", then the matter is settled by deliberation. Nāmadeva, having won Indra over by abundant praise, made such an agreement in the Ṛk *iṃdraṃ ka imam*; hence the two Ṛks have one sense, "bhūyasā vasnam". Sāyaṇa then explains: someone obtains (acarat) a very small price (vasnam) for great goods; going back to the buyer he says "it was not sold by me" and desires (akāniṣam, with the person changed) the completion of the price. That seller does not drain the buyer (nārirecīt) of a smaller sum by a greater amount, that is, does not obtain it. Helpless and skilful alike (dīnāḥ, dakṣāḥ) draw out (vi pra duhanti) the word as spoken, getting at the time of purchase exactly what was said. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भूयसा (*bhūyasā*)—for an article of great value
- कनीयः (*kanīyaḥ*)—very small
- वस्यं (*vasyaṃ*)—price
- अचरत् (*acarat*)—(the seller) obtains
- पुनर्यन् (*punaryan*)—(after realizing it was a loss to him) going again to the buyer

### Printed p. 147
- अविक्रीतः (*avikrītaḥ*)—saying "I have not sold you my article at all"
- अकानिषं (*akāniṣaṃ*)—(asks) for the balance due to the worth of the article
- सः (*saḥ*)—the buyer
- भूयसा कनीयः नारिरेचित् (*bhūyasā kanīyaḥ nārirecit*)—by giving more money, does not make up the small sum (paid before). Or: does not give back the valuable article received and recover from the seller the small sum he had paid
- दीनाः (*dīnāḥ*)—whether helpless
- दक्षाः (*dakṣāḥ*)—or capable
- वाणं (*vāṇaṃ*)—according to the word they spoke
- वि प्र दुहंति (*vi pra duhaṃti*)—must get just the price agreed at the time of purchase

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

A seller, whether from haste or ignorance, sells an article of great value at a small price; afterwards, becoming aware of its worth, he comes to the buyer and says "I have not sold you my article", and demands either his article or the balance due according to its worth. But the buyer does not wish to give back such a valuable article and get again a small sum. Whether capable or incapable, people must get only the price they fixed by speaking at the time of purchase.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
A man has realized a small price for an article of great value, and again coming (to the buyer he says) this has not been sold ; I require the full price; but he does not recover a small price by a large (equivalent): whether helpless or clever they adhere to their bargain.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Regarding this Ṛk and the next, the commentator says that traditional earlier teachers recite certain verses, and he gives those verses here:
अल्पं यः परिगृह्णाति मूल्यं पण्येन भूयसा । स क्रेतारं पुनर्गच्छन्न विक्रीतस्त्वयं मया ॥ १ ॥ (*alpaṃ yaḥ parigṛhṇāti mūlyaṃ paṇyena bhūyasā | sa kretāraṃ punargacchanna vikrītastvayaṃ mayā ‖ 1 ‖*)
इति ब्रुवन् कामयते पुनर्मूल्यस्य पूरणं । स विक्रेता पुनर्मूल्यं भूयसा न प्रपूरयेत् ॥ २ ॥ (*iti bruvan kāmayate punarmūlyasya pūraṇaṃ | sa vikretā punarmūlyaṃ bhūyasā na prapūrayet ‖ 2 ‖*)
हीनं न लभते वस्नं यदा विक्रीतवान् पुरा । यथा समयमेव स्यात्तयोर्न पुनरन्यथा ॥ ३ ॥ (*hīnaṃ na labhate vasnaṃ yadā vikrītavān purā | yathā samayameva syāttayorna punaranyathā ‖ 3 ‖*)
अयं विक्रय एवेति समयश्चेत्कृतो भवेत् । अथ मूल्यार्थमेतत्त्वाद्विद्विचार्यैव तु निर्णयः ॥ ४ ॥ (*ayaṃ vikraya eveti samayaścetkṛto bhavet | atha mūlyārthametattvādvidvicāryaiva tu nirṇayaḥ ‖ 4 ‖*)

### Printed p. 148
इत्येवं समयोऽकारि तदा मूल्यं प्रपूर्यते । तस्मादादौ मया कार्यः समयोऽत्रेति चिंतयन् ॥ ५ ॥ (*ityevaṃ samayo 'kāri tadā mūlyaṃ prapūryate | tasmādādau mayā kāryaḥ samayo 'treti ciṃtayan ‖ 5 ‖*)
नामदेवो वशीकृत्य शक्रं स्तोत्रेण भूयसा । विक्रीणन् समयं चक्र इंद्रं क इममित्यृचि ॥ ६ ॥ (*nāmadevo vaśīkṛtya śakraṃ stotreṇa bhūyasā | vikrīṇan samayaṃ cakra iṃdraṃ ka imamityṛci ‖ 6 ‖*)
अतश्च द्वृच एकार्थो भूयसा वस्यमित्ययं ॥ इति ॥ (*ataśca dvṛca ekārtho bhūyasā vasyamityayaṃ ‖ iti ‖*)
This Ṛk and the next together suggest one idea. The verses above make it clear through an example. Thus: one man, a trader, sells an article to another man. The man who buys the article asks and learns its price at that time, settles it, gives the price fixed by the seller and takes the article away. Then after a little while the trader thinks: "The article I sold is very valuable. It was unjust to sell it at a small price. I ought to get a higher price." Having thought so, he goes to the buyer and says: "O man, the price of the article you bought is far too low. So now give me a higher price for it." But the buyer says to the trader: "I bought it by paying the price you named. I have done no wrong. At the time of the trade I agreed to give the price you asked and paid it accordingly; so it was an agreement made between the two of us. We both must keep it." The trader, pushing justice aside, shows roughness: "I have not sold you the article at all. Give it back to me." Then the buyer replies: "O trader, if the article was worth more, you should have asked a higher price at the very time of selling. If I liked it I would have taken it; if not, not. To leave that and ask more now is not just, and it is not possible for me to give it. Trade is an agreement between two, the seller and the buyer. Both must keep it; neither may transgress it."
The chief point to note here is that once an agreement has been made in a trade between seller and buyer, both must act accordingly. If the seller, not seeing the high price of the article at that time, sold it, it is wrong afterwards to ask a higher price. Profit or loss, the seller must bear it. Likewise the buyer too, saying that the article he obtained is not very valuable and that the price paid was too high, asking that the price be returned

### Printed p. 149
or reduced, is also wrong. Since the question whether the price is high or low was settled at the time of trade, or is deemed settled, it is not just for either of the two to ask or give a higher price. The principle is stated that both must bear profit or loss.
What is said in the present Ṛks (especially in the tenth Ṛk) follows this principle. Thus: the seer Vāmadeva had already propitiated Indra with one or two hymns of praise. That being so, it is not right for another man (a sacrificer) to praise Indra with ten hymns and claim Indra as his own. For by the principle stated above, the seer Vāmadeva praised Indra first. Though his hymns may be fewer, since he praised before the other sacrificer, Indra should first favour the seer Vāmadeva. There is no reason why the new sacrificer who praised with more hymns should have a greater claim on Indra's favour. Therefore Vāmadeva makes this agreement with the new sacrificer, the man who praised with ten hymns: "O devotee of Indra, since I praised this Indra first, before you, this Indra is mine. You have won him over for the time being with more praisers (ten hymns). Therefore, in return for your hymns, as soon as Indra has destroyed your enemies he must be sent back again to me, who praised him first." This is the agreement. This very matter is explained in these two Ṛks by the illustration of trade.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क इ॒मं द॒शभि॒र्ममेंद्रं॑ क्रीणाति धे॒नुभिः॑ ।  
> य॒दा वृ॒त्राणि॒ जंघ॑न॒दथै॑नं मे॒ पुन॑र्ददत् ॥ १० ॥

> *ka imaṃ daśabhirmameṃdraṃ krīṇāti dhenubhiḥ |*  
> *yadā vṛtrāṇi jaṃghanadathainaṃ me punardadat ‖ 10 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कः । इमं । दशभिः । मम । इंद्रं । क्रीणाति । धेनुभिः ॥  
> यदा । वृत्राणि । जंघनत् । अथ । एनं । मे । पुनः । ददत् ॥ १० ॥

> *kaḥ | imaṃ | daśabhiḥ | mama | iṃdraṃ | krīṇāti | dhenubhiḥ ‖*  
> *yadā | vṛtrāṇi | jaṃghanat | atha | enaṃ | me | punaḥ | dadat ‖ 10 ‖*

### Printed p. 150

> मम मदीयमिमं स्वभूतमिंद्रं धेनुभिः प्रीणयित्रीभिर्दशभिर्दशसंख्याकाभिः स्तुतिभिः कः क्रीणाति । क्रयं करोति । तदानीं हे क्रेतारो युष्माकं मध्य
> एवमपि समयः क्रियते । यदायमिंद्रो वृत्राणि त्वदीयान् शत्रुन् जंघनत्
> हन्यात् अथानंतरमेवैनमिंद्रं मे मह्यं पुनर्ददत् । पुनर्दद्यात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: who buys this Indra, who is "mine" (my own), with ten milch cows, that is, with ten gratifying hymns of praise? Who makes this purchase? Then the buyers are told that a compact is made among them: when this Indra shall slay your foes (वृत्राणि (*vṛtrāṇi*), "your enemies"), immediately thereafter let him give Indra back to me.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मम इमं (*mama imaṃ*)—the one whom (by praise and the like) I have made my own
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- धेनुभिः (*dhenubhiḥ*)—with those that are gratifying
- दशभिः (*daśabhiḥ*)—with hymns of praise, ten in number
- कः (*kaḥ*)—which devotee
- क्रीणाति (*krīṇāti*)—buys (for help). (With such a worshipper I make the following compact, namely:)
- यदा (*yadā*)—when
- वृत्राणि (*vṛtrāṇi*)—the enemies (of the worshipper who has bought Indra for a time)
- जंघनत् (*jaṃghanat*)—he destroys
- अथ (*atha*)—immediately after that
- एनं (*enaṃ*)—this Indra
- मे (*me*)—to me alone
- पुनर्ददत् (*punardadat*)—he must be sent back again

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

With the devotee who buys, for help, this Indra whom I have made my own by praise and the like, with gratifying hymns ten in number, I make the following compact: "O devotee of Indra, this Indra is mine. You have bought him only for a time. As soon as he has destroyed your enemies, you must send him back to me." That is the compact.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Who buys this, my Indra, with ten milch cows? when he shall have slain (your) foes, then let (the purchaser) give him again to me.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Since the 9th and 10th Ṛks are related to each other, the special points suited to both Ṛks together were explained under the 9th Ṛk itself. No separate explanation is needed for this Ṛk.

### Printed p. 151
#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नू ष्टु॒त इं॑द्र॒ नू गृ॑णा॒न इषं॑ जरि॒त्रे न॒द्यो॒३॒॑३ न पी॑पेः ।  
> अका॑रि ते हरिवो॒ ब्रह्म॒ नव्यं॑ धि॒या स्या॑म र॒थ्यः॑ सदा॒साः ॥ ११ ॥

> *nū ṣṭuta iṃdra nū gṛṇāna iṣaṃ jaritre nadyo3 na pīpeḥ |*  
> *akāri te harivo brahma navyaṃ dhiyā syāma rathyaḥ sadāsāḥ ‖ 11 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नु । स्तुतः । इंद्र । नु । गृणानः । इषं । जरित्रे । नद्यः । न । पीपेरिति पीपेः ।  
> अकारि । ते । हरिवः । ब्रह्म । नव्यं । धिया । स्याम । रथ्यः । सदासाः ॥ ११ ॥

> *nu | stutaḥ | iṃdra | nu | gṛṇānaḥ | iṣaṃ | jaritre | nadyaḥ | na | pīperiti pīpeḥ |*  
> *akāri | te | harivaḥ | brahma | navyaṃ | dhiyā | syāma | rathyaḥ | sadāsāḥ ‖ 11 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र नु क्षिप्रं स्तुतः पूर्वैर्यषिभिः संस्तुतो नु क्षिप्रं गृणानोऽस्माभिः
> स्तूयमानस्त्वमिषमन्नं जरित्रे स्तोत्रे मह्यं नद्यो नाद्भिनदीरिव पीपेः ।
> प्यायय ! प्रवृद्धं कुरु ! हे हरिवो हरिसंज्ञ काश्वोपेतेंद्र ते तुभ्यं नव्यं
> नवतरं ब्रह्म स्तोत्रमकारि ! अस्माभिः क्रियते ! रथ्यो रथवंतो वयं धिया
> प्रज्ञारूपया स्तुत्या सदासास्त्वां सर्वदा भजमानास्त्वदर्थं हवीरूपस्यान्नस्य
> दातारो वा स्याम ! भूयास्मै ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you who were quickly (नु (*nu*) = quickly) praised by the earlier seers and are now quickly praised by us: fill up food for me, the worshipper (जरित्रे (*jaritre*) = the praiser), as rivers are filled with water; make it abundant. O lord of the horses called Hari, a newer hymn (ब्रह्म (*brahma*) = stotra) has been made for you by us. May we, possessed of chariots, with praise in the form of insight (धिया (*dhiyā*)), always serving you, become givers of oblation-food for you.
